s=open('stone.py').read()
a=s.index('# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the painter')
b=s.index('def contact_under')
new='''# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the painter
DEFAULT = dict(face=(2.0, 2.9), face_clip=(2.15, 2.88), lump=0.15, lump_px=2.5,
               wmax=0.2, cres_cut=0.7, cres_down=0.35, u8=0.45, u8b=0.56, uB=0.75, uBD=0.9,
               jitter=0.12, crown_rows=2, top_step=False, seed=0,
               turn=(0.22, 0.12, 0.05),      # N.L below which the lit face walks down: TURN/FD, TURN, EDGE
               term=0.0,                      # N.L of the terminator (lit family | shadow family)
               crescent=True, rim=True)


def paint_stone(out, m, L, dark_nb=None, N=None, classes=(EDGE, TURN, FD, FL),
                shadow_classes=(CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT), P=None):
    """paint one stone mask `m` into the step canvas `out`.
    L: light direction in screen space (x right, y DOWN, z toward viewer).
    N: per-pixel screen normals (default: ellipsoid dome fitted to the silhouette).
    dark_nb: bool map of darker things around (for the reflected rim); default: all non-stone.
    classes: the lit family [edge/core, turn, face-dark, face-light];
    shadow_classes: the shadow family [contact, core, shadow, reflected]."""
    P = {**DEFAULT, **(P or {})}
    rng = np.random.default_rng(P['seed'])
    L = np.asarray(L, float); L = L / np.linalg.norm(L)
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    size = min(np.ptp(xs), np.ptp(ys)) + 1
    if N is None:
        N = dome_normals(m, P.get('squash', 1.0))
    s = N @ L
    lit = s > P['term']
    sm = s[m & lit] if (m & lit).any() else s[m]
    hi, lo = np.percentile(sm, 97), np.percentile(sm, 20)
    t = np.clip((s - lo) / max(hi - lo, 1e-6), 0, 1)
    f0, f1 = P['face']
    x = f0 + (f1 - f0) * t
    if size >= 8 and P['lump'] > 0:
        x = x + P['lump'] * smooth_noise(m.shape, rng, P['lump_px'])
    x = np.clip(x, *P['face_clip'])
    # the lit surface turning toward the terminator walks down through the lit family
    ta, tb, tc = P['turn']
    x = np.where(s < ta, np.minimum(x, 1.5), x)
    x = np.where(s < tb, np.minimum(x, 1.0), x)
    x = np.where(s < tc, np.minimum(x, 0.0), x)
    # ---- crescent (pixels from the silhouette, opposite the light)
    o = outward(m)
    L2 = L[:2] / max(np.linalg.norm(L[:2]), 1e-6)
    if P['crescent'] and size >= 6:
        d = ndi.distance_transform_edt(m) - 1.0
        cres = P.get('cres_dir')
        c2 = -L2 if cres is None else np.asarray(cres, float)
        c2 = c2 + np.array([0.0, P['cres_down']])
        c2 = c2 / np.linalg.norm(c2)
        cc = o @ c2
        wmax = P['wmax'] * size
        w = wmax * np.clip((cc - P['cres_cut']) / (1 - P['cres_cut']), 0, 1)
        jit = P['jitter'] * (1.0 if size >= 16 else 0.3)
        u = d / np.maximum(w, 1e-6) + jit * smooth_noise(m.shape, rng, 0.7) * 2.2
        prof = np.select([u < P['u8'], u < P['u8b'], u < P['uB'], u < P['uBD']], [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5], default=9.0)
        x = np.minimum(x, np.where(w > 0.9, prof, 9.0))
    e_dn = edge_dist_down(m)
    e_up = edge_dist_up(m)
    # ---- crown
    x = np.where((e_up < P['crown_rows']) & (x >= 2), 3.0, x)
    # ---- rim
    ring = m & ~ndi.binary_erosion(m, CROSS)
    ring2 = m & ~ndi.binary_erosion(m, CROSS, iterations=2) & ~ring
    if dark_nb is None:
        dark_nb = ~m
    ca = o @ L2
    down = o[..., 1]
    nb_dark = ndi.binary_dilation(dark_nb & ~m, CROSS) & m
    nb_light = ndi.binary_dilation(~dark_nb & ~m, CROSS) & m
    upper = (down < 0.35) & (x >= 2)
    x = np.where((ring | ring2) & ndi.binary_dilation(ring & nb_light & upper, CROSS, iterations=1) & upper, 3.0, x)
    if P['rim'] and size >= 10:
        away_rim = ring & nb_dark & (ca < -0.1) & (e_dn > 0) & lit
        rv = np.clip(3.0 - 2.2 * np.clip(down + 0.3, 0, 1.3), 1.0, 3.0)
        x = np.where(away_rim, np.minimum(x + 1, np.round(rv)), x)
        inner = ring2 & ndi.binary_dilation(away_rim, CROSS) & lit
        x = np.where(inner, np.minimum(x, np.where(down < 0.0, 1.0, 0.0)), x)
    # ---- bottom
    x = np.where(e_dn == 0, 0.0, x)
    x = np.where(e_dn == 1, np.minimum(x, 1.0 if size >= 6 else 0.0), x)
    v = ordered(x, classes)
    # ---- shadow family on the side turned from the light
    if (m & ~lit).any():
        # core just past the terminator; reflected light where the surface faces the lit ground
        bounce = np.clip(0.6 * N[..., 1] + 0.4 * (N[..., :2] @ -L2), 0, 1)     # faces down/away
        xs_ = 2.0 + 1.0 * bounce - 1.0 * np.exp(-((s - P['term']) / 0.12) ** 2) * (1 - bounce)
        xs_ = np.where(e_dn == 0, 0.6, xs_)
        vs = ordered(np.clip(xs_, 0, 3), shadow_classes)
        v = np.where(lit, v, vs)
    if P['top_step']:
        v = np.where((s > np.percentile(sm, 96)) & (e_up > 0) & (v == classes[3]), TOP, v)
    out[m] = v[m]
    return v

"""stage1.py -- one cobble in greyscale: planes, two light families, core, reflected light,
contact and cast shadow, from 40 px down to 4 px, under several lights."""
import sys

import numpy as np
from stone import *
from util import *

SUNS = {'sun behind-left, 40 deg': (140, 40), 'sun behind-right, 50 deg': (40, 50),
        'sun front-left, 35 deg': (215, 35), 'sun low left, 15 deg': (170, 15)}
SIZES = [40, 24, 16, 10, 6, 4]


def one(D, sun, seed=2, P=None, geo=None):
    g = cobble_geom(D, sun=sun, seed=seed, **(geo or {}))
    m, N, L = g['mask'], g['N'], g['L']

    ground = ~m
    paint_ground(out, ground)
    sh = g['shadow'] & ~m
    paint_shadow_on_ground(out, g['shadow'], m)
    paint_stone(out, m, L, dark_nb=sh, N=N, P=P)
    contact_under(out, m)

## Round 1 — 2026-09-25 21:22 — stage 0 master copy, `img/stage0_copy_v3.png` (V19 lit boulder)
**Verdict: REVISE** — close in drawing, not yet indistinguishable at 8×. (My read; you own the call. The bar I'm judging against is "a skilled pixel artist can't tell your crop from his at 8× except by subject".)

What's working:

- The main boulder's silhouette, its size, and the lit/shadow split are right. The dark lower rim sitting under the lit dome reads as a rounded stone at a glance.
- The snow wedges (white with a 1 px blue rim on stepped diagonals) are nearly his.

What gives it away, in order of how fast the eye catches it:

1. **The dither on the boulder is one uniform checker over the whole dome.** In his crop the checker is *patchy*: the upper-left crown is mostly flat mid-orange with only scattered checker, the checker thickens into the core toward the lower right, and there are 2–3 px flat islands inside it. Yours has the same 50% density edge to edge, which is exactly the "mechanical checker" the master-copy student hit in C1. Let density carry the turn (sparse on the crown, dense toward the terminator), and break it with small flat islands.
2. **The rim is a clean continuous ring.** His dark lower rim is chunky and uneven: 2–3 px thick at lower left, thinning to 1 px up the right side, with notches where the next stone overlaps. Yours runs at an even width, like an outline, and a mid-brown band sits inside it all the way round. That band is the most "drawn by a program" part of the copy.
3. **The shadow field (lower left) is one uniform dark checker.** His is solid near-black pockets, checker zones, and diagonal clusters of the lighter dark, all in patches. Pockets of solid dark where stones touch are what make the pile read as a pile; yours reads as one flat dark material.
4. **Top-left and top-right stones.** His top-left is a jumble of small lit and dark faceted chips; your top band is a blank mid strip. His top-right stone carries short dark crack ticks (2–4 px diagonal marks) in its lit face; yours is smooth.

Next, the single most useful thing: fix (1) and (3) together. Both are "one dither density everywhere", and both are what separates a copy from a fit.
Write: elements/cobbles/materials.py
"""materials.py -- what makes a painted cobble read as granite, greywacke or basalt (stage 2).

All marks are OFFSETS IN STEP SPACE on top of the form (paint_stone's output), in object space
(they stick to the stone), and every mark is one step up or down its own family -- never a
new value.  The value family of the whole stone also shifts with the rock (granite light,
greywacke mid, basalt dark), because at pixel scale albedo is the first thing that reads.

  granite    speckle: isolated 1-px flecks, mostly dark (biotite/hornblende), a few light
             (feldspar), GATHERED in patches by a noise field, dense in the light, rare in
             shadow; the lit face dither is sparser (granite is matte and grainy, the grain IS
             the texture).  Edge: slightly knobbly silhouette.
  greywacke  smooth and fine: no speckle; instead 0-2 QUARTZ VEINS, 1-px clean-run lines one or
             two steps lighter crossing the stone in a straight/2:1 line and wrapping over the
             edge; mid value, a bit of cool mottle (big lumps).  Edge: smooth.
  basalt     dark, dense, fine: the whole family shifted a step down, almost no marks, a few
             dark vesicle pits; being smooth and dark it carries the crispest sun-glint
             (1-3 px TOP cluster) -- the value contrast of a dark smooth surface.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from stone import (paint_stone, smooth_noise, edge_dist_down, edge_dist_up, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH,
                   SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP, CROSS)

ROCKS = {
    'granite':   dict(classes=(EDGE, TURN, FD, FL), shadow=(CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT),
                      P=dict(face=(2.2, 3.0), face_clip=(2.3, 3.0), lump=0.12), lump_geo=0.14),
    'greywacke': dict(classes=(SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD), shadow=(CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT),
                      P=dict(face=(2.0, 2.9), face_clip=(2.05, 2.9), lump=0.22, lump_px=4.0), lump_geo=0.08),
    'basalt':    dict(classes=(SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN), shadow=(CONTACT, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH),
                      P=dict(face=(2.0, 2.8), face_clip=(2.0, 2.8), lump=0.1), lump_geo=0.07),
}
RAMP_ORDER = [CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP]


def step_up(v, k=1):
    i = np.searchsorted(RAMP_ORDER, v)
    return np.asarray(RAMP_ORDER)[np.clip(i + k, 0, len(RAMP_ORDER) - 1)]


def granite_speckle(v, m, lit, rng, size, density=0.10):
    if size < 8:
        return v
    patch = smooth_noise(m.shape, rng, max(1.5, size / 10)) > -0.2          # where grains gather
    r = rng.random(m.shape)
    e_dn = edge_dist_down(m)
    interior = m & ndi.binary_erosion(m, CROSS) & (e_dn > 1)
    dens = np.where(lit, density, density * 0.35)
    dark = interior & patch & (r < dens * 0.75)
    light = interior & patch & (r > 1 - dens * 0.25) & lit
    # isolated flecks only: no two flecks side by side (a speck is one pixel)
    dark &= ~(np.roll(dark, 1, 0) | np.roll(dark, 1, 1))
    v = np.where(dark, step_up(v, -1 if size < 16 else -2), v)
    v = np.where(light & ~dark, step_up(v, 1), v)
    return v


def clean_line(m, rng, slope_choices=((1, 0), (2, 1), (1, 1), (3, 1), (1, 2))):
    """a straight clean-run line across the mask (vein): run/rise from clean ratios."""
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    cy, cx = ys.mean() + rng.normal() * np.ptp(ys) * 0.2, xs.mean() + rng.normal() * np.ptp(xs) * 0.25
    run, rise = slope_choices[rng.integers(len(slope_choices))]
    sgn = 1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1
    line = np.zeros_like(m)
    L = int(np.ptp(xs) + np.ptp(ys)) + 4
    for k in range(-L, L):
        # step along the line in whole-pixel runs
        x = int(round(cx + k * run / max(run, rise)))
        y = int(round(cy + sgn * k * rise / max(run, rise)))
        if 0 <= y < m.shape[0] and 0 <= x < m.shape[1]:
            line[y, x] = True
    return line & m


def greywacke_veins(v, m, rng, size, n=None):
    if size < 10:
        return v
    n = n if n is not None else rng.choice([0, 1, 1, 2])
    for _ in range(n):
        ln = clean_line(m, rng)
        ln &= edge_dist_down(m) > 0
        v = np.where(ln, step_up(v, 2), v)
    return v


def basalt_marks(v, m, lit, s, rng, size):
    if size >= 10:
        r = rng.random(m.shape)
        pit = m & ndi.binary_erosion(m, CROSS) & (r < 0.025)
        v = np.where(pit, step_up(v, -1), v)
    # sun glint: the few pixels facing the light most squarely go to TOP (smooth dark rock)
    if size >= 6 and lit.any():
        thr = np.percentile(s[m & lit], 98.5 if size >= 16 else 96)
        g = m & lit & (s >= thr) & (edge_dist_up(m) > 0)
        v = np.where(g, np.maximum(step_up(v, 2), v), v)
    return v


def paint_rock(out, m, L, rock, N=None, dark_nb=None, seed=0, P=None):
    R = ROCKS[rock]
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 101)
    PP = {**R['P'], 'seed': seed, **(P or {})}
    v = paint_stone(out, m, L, dark_nb=dark_nb, N=N, classes=R['classes'], shadow_classes=R['shadow'], P=PP)
    if N is None:
        from stone import dome_normals
        N = dome_normals(m)
    Ln = np.asarray(L, float) / np.linalg.norm(L)
    s = N @ Ln
    lit = s > 0
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    size = min(np.ptp(xs), np.ptp(ys)) + 1
    if rock == 'granite':
        v = granite_speckle(v, m, lit, rng, size)
    elif rock == 'greywacke':
        v = greywacke_veins(v, m, rng, size)
    elif rock == 'basalt':
        v = basalt_marks(v, m, lit, s, rng, size)
    out[m] = v[m]
    return v

"""stage2.py -- the cobble's material in greyscale: granite, greywacke, basalt."""
import sys

from materials import paint_rock, ROCKS

          'greywacke': (R + 'braided_cobbles_hope.jpg', (860, 425, 1050, 560)),

def one(D, sun, rock, seed=2, geo=None):

    return out

Write: elements/cobbles/palette.py
"""palette.py -- the palette is the light (stage 3).

One 9-step ramp per (rock, hour, wetness): steps are the same indices stone.py paints
  0 CONTACT 1 SH_DARK 2 SH 3 SH_LIGHT | 4 EDGE 5 TURN 6 FD 7 FL 8 TOP
Each ramp is a HUE WALK in Lab, not a lightness scale of one hue:

  * the shadow family takes the SKY's hue (cool blue-violet at midday, violet at dusk),
  * the lit family takes the SUN's hue (neutral-warm at noon, orange at golden hour),
  * the rock's own albedo tint (granite pink-grey, greywacke green-grey, basalt blue-black)
    sits under both, strongest in the halftones (TURN/FD), where the light is least coloured,
  * chroma peaks in the halftones and falls toward black and the top light (Ferrari's rock
    ramps: saturated middle, greyed ends).
Lightness per step comes from the rock's albedo (granite lightest, basalt darkest) with the
lit/shadow GAP kept at the terminator (EDGE is always clearly above SH_LIGHT).
Wet: everything darker and more saturated, the TOP step becomes a near-white glint.
"""
import numpy as np


def lab_to_rgb(lab):
    lab = np.asarray(lab, float)
    L, a, b = lab[..., 0], lab[..., 1], lab[..., 2]
    fy = (L + 16) / 116; fx = fy + a / 500; fz = fy - b / 200
    def finv(t):
        return np.where(t > 6 / 29, t ** 3, 3 * (6 / 29) ** 2 * (t - 4 / 29))
    X = 0.9505 * finv(fx); Y = finv(fy); Z = 1.089 * finv(fz)
    M = np.array([[3.2406, -1.5372, -0.4986], [-0.9689, 1.8758, 0.0415], [0.0557, -0.2040, 1.0570]])
    rgb = np.stack([X, Y, Z], -1) @ M.T
    rgb = np.where(rgb > 0.0031308, 1.055 * np.clip(rgb, 0, None) ** (1 / 2.4) - 0.055, 12.92 * rgb)
    return np.clip(np.round(rgb * 255), 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)


def rgb_to_lab(rgb):
    a = np.asarray(rgb, float) / 255
    a = np.where(a > 0.04045, ((a + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4, a / 12.92)
    M = np.array([[0.4124, 0.3576, 0.1805], [0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722], [0.0193, 0.1192, 0.9505]])
    xyz = a @ M.T / np.array([0.9505, 1.0, 1.089])
    f = np.where(xyz > 0.008856, np.cbrt(xyz), 7.787 * xyz + 16 / 116)
    return np.stack([116 * f[..., 1] - 16, 500 * (f[..., 0] - f[..., 1]), 200 * (f[..., 1] - f[..., 2])], -1)


def hue(deg, c):
    return np.array([c * np.cos(np.radians(deg)), c * np.sin(np.radians(deg))])


# rock albedo: lightness of the lit face (FL) and shadow (SH), own tint (hue deg, chroma)
ROCK = {'granite':   dict(L_fl=74, L_sh=30, tint=(40, 7)),      # pink-grey
        'greywacke': dict(L_fl=62, L_sh=25, tint=(150, 6)),     # green-grey
        'basalt':    dict(L_fl=46, L_sh=16, tint=(260, 5)),     # blue-black
        'sandstone': dict(L_fl=70, L_sh=30, tint=(65, 16))}

# light: sun hue/chroma on the lit family, sky hue/chroma on the shadow family, contrast scale
HOUR = {'noon':     dict(sun=(80, 6), sky=(265, 9), gap=1.0, top=1.0),
        'morning':  dict(sun=(70, 12), sky=(255, 12), gap=1.05, top=1.0),
        'golden':   dict(sun=(60, 26), sky=(290, 14), gap=1.2, top=1.05),
        'dusk':     dict(sun=(20, 18), sky=(305, 16), gap=0.8, top=0.85),
        'overcast': dict(sun=(230, 3), sky=(235, 6), gap=0.55, top=0.9)}


def ramp(rock='granite', hour='noon', wet=0.0):
    r, h = ROCK[rock], HOUR[hour]
    Lfl, Lsh = r['L_fl'], r['L_sh']
    # lightness per step: shadow family below Lsh+, lit family from EDGE up to TOP
    g = h['gap']
    Lsh_scale = Lsh * (1.0 if g >= 1 else 1 + (1 - g) * 0.9)     # overcast: shadows lift
    Ls = [Lsh_scale * 0.35, Lsh_scale * 0.62, Lsh_scale * 0.85, Lsh_scale * 1.08]
    span = Lfl - Ls[-1]
    Ll = [Ls[-1] + span * f for f in (0.28, 0.55, 0.78, 1.0)] + [min(97, Lfl + 12 * h['top'])]
    Ls[-1] = min(Ls[-1], Ll[0] - 5)
    Lv = np.array(Ls + Ll)
    # wet stones: darker (water fills the pores), more saturated, glint on top
    if wet > 0:
        Lv[:8] = Lv[:8] * (1 - 0.3 * wet)
    # chroma envelope: peak in the halftones
    env = np.array([0.25, 0.45, 0.7, 0.9, 0.85, 1.0, 0.9, 0.7, 0.35]) * (1 + 0.6 * wet)
    ab = []
    for i in range(9):
        own = hue(*r['tint']) * (1.2 if i in (4, 5, 6) else 0.8)
        light = hue(*h['sun']) if i >= 4 else hue(*h['sky'])
        # the reflected step picks up the lit gravel (a touch of the sun hue)
        if i == 3:
            light = 0.6 * hue(*h['sky']) + 0.4 * hue(*h['sun'])
        ab.append((own + light) * env[i])
    ab = np.array(ab)
    lab = np.concatenate([Lv[:, None], ab], 1)
    rgb = lab_to_rgb(lab)
    if wet > 0:
        rgb[8] = lab_to_rgb(np.array([min(98, Lfl + 24), *(hue(*h['sun']) * 0.4)]))
    return [tuple(int(c) for c in x) for x in rgb]


def ground_ramp(hour='noon'):
    """the gravel between cobbles: a mix of all rocks, one mid-grey hue walk."""
    return ramp('greywacke', hour)

"""stage3.py -- colour: a deliberate hue-walk ramp per rock type, light as hue shift."""
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, __file__.rsplit('/', 1)[0])
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from stone import *
from materials import paint_rock, ROCKS
from palette import ramp, ground_ramp, HOUR
from util import *

SUN_BY_HOUR = {'noon': (120, 62), 'morning': (30, 30), 'golden': (160, 16), 'dusk': (175, 6), 'overcast': (100, 80)}


def render(D, rock, hour, seed=2, wet=0.0):
    g = cobble_geom(D, sun=SUN_BY_HOUR[hour], seed=seed, lump=ROCKS[rock]['lump_geo'])
    m, N, L = g['mask'], g['N'], g['L']
    out = np.zeros(m.shape, int)
    paint_ground(out, ~m)
    if hour != 'overcast':
        paint_shadow_on_ground(out, g['shadow'], m)
    paint_rock(out, m, L, rock, N=N, dark_nb=g['shadow'] & ~m, seed=seed)
    if D >= 9:
        contact_under(out, m)
    rp = np.array(ramp(rock, hour, wet), np.uint8)
    gp = np.array(ground_ramp(hour), np.uint8)
    rgb = np.where(m[..., None], rp[out], gp[out])
    return rgb


def swatch(pal, s=18):
    im = Image.new('RGB', (s * len(pal), s))
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    for i, c in enumerate(pal):
        d.rectangle([i * s, 0, i * s + s - 1, s - 1], fill=c)
    return im

Write: elements/cobbles/scene.py
"""scene.py -- several cobbles composed: nestled, overlapping, separated by value (stage 4+).

Canvas = two int layers: `step` (0..8, the stone.py steps) and `mat` (-1 ground, else rock id),
plus `wet` (0/1) so each pixel is rendered with ramp(rock, hour, wet)[step].

Composition rules (from Ferrari's V19 pile and c_stones shore):

  * back to front (by base row); a nearer stone simply overwrites.
  * CAST SHADOW: a stone's footprint along the sun falls on the ground AND on neighbours;
    shadowed stone pixels are repainted in the shadow family (they keep their form, lose the sun).
  * CREVICE: where a nearer stone's silhouette overlaps a farther stone, the farther stone's pixels
    along that edge drop into a dark pocket (1-2 px, darkest right at the join) -- stones separate
    by VALUE, never by an outline drawn round the nearer one.
  * POCKETS: ground crowded by stones is solid CONTACT, opening into checker.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from stone import (cobble_geom, paint_stone, ordered, shift, edge_dist_down, CROSS, CONTACT, SH_DARK,
                   SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP, convex_clean)
from materials import paint_rock, ROCKS
from palette import ramp, ground_ramp

ROCK_IDS = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(['granite', 'greywacke', 'basalt', 'sandstone'])}
ROCK_NAMES = {v: k for k, v in ROCK_IDS.items()}


class Canvas:
    def __init__(self, W, H):
        self.W, self.H = W, H
        self.step = np.full((H, W), TURN, int)
        self.mat = np.full((H, W), -1, int)
        self.wet = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
        self.zid = np.full((H, W), -1, int)       # which stone owns the pixel
        self.shadow = np.zeros((H, W), bool)      # cast-shadow footprint on anything
        self.water = np.zeros((H, W), bool)

    def render(self, hour='noon', ground=None, water_pal=None):
        out = np.zeros((self.H, self.W, 3), np.uint8)
        gp = np.array(ground if ground is not None else ground_ramp(hour), np.uint8)
        g = self.mat < 0
        out[g] = gp[self.step[g]]
        for name, rid in ROCK_IDS.items():
            for w in (0, 1):
                sel = (self.mat == rid) & (self.wet == w)
                if sel.any():
                    rp = np.array(ramp(name, hour, wet=0.8 * w), np.uint8)
                    out[sel] = rp[self.step[sel]]
        if water_pal is not None and self.water.any():
            wp = np.array(water_pal, np.uint8)
            out[self.water] = wp[np.clip(self.step[self.water], 0, len(wp) - 1)]
        return out


def place(cv, stones, sun=(140, 40), pitch=45.0, P=None):
    """stones: list of dict(x, y, D, rock, seed, [elong, flat, yaw, wet]); (x, y) = screen position
    of the stone's base centre.  Painted back to front."""
    geoms = []
    for i, s in enumerate(stones):
        g = cobble_geom(s['D'], sun=sun, pitch=pitch, seed=s.get('seed', i),
                        elong=s.get('elong', 1.35), flat=s.get('flat', 0.65),
                        lump=s.get('lump', ROCKS[s['rock']]['lump_geo']), yaw=s.get('yaw', 0.0),
                        sink=s.get('sink', 0.3), pad=2)
        m = g['mask']
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
        ox = int(round(s['x'] - (xs.min() + xs.max()) / 2))
        oy = int(round(s['y'] - ys.max()))
        geoms.append((s, g, ox, oy))
    order = sorted(range(len(stones)), key=lambda i: stones[i]['y'])
    # scene-space masks
    def to_scene(a, ox, oy):
        A = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), bool)
        h, w = a.shape
        y0, x0 = max(0, oy), max(0, ox)
        y1, x1 = min(cv.H, oy + h), min(cv.W, ox + w)
        if y1 > y0 and x1 > x0:
            A[y0:y1, x0:x1] = a[y0 - oy:y1 - oy, x0 - ox:x1 - ox]
        return A
    masks = {i: to_scene(geoms[i][1]['mask'], geoms[i][2], geoms[i][3]) for i in order}
    shads = {i: to_scene(geoms[i][1]['shadow'], geoms[i][2], geoms[i][3]) for i in order}
    Lw = geoms[0][1]['Lw']
    sund = np.array([Lw[0], Lw[1]]) / max(np.hypot(Lw[0], Lw[1]), 1e-6)
    anyst = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), bool)
    for i in order:
        anyst |= masks[i]
    # visible part of each stone after nearer stones overwrite
    vis = {}
    cover = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), bool)
    for i in reversed(order):
        vis[i] = masks[i] & ~cover
        cover |= masks[i]
    for i in order:
        s, g, ox, oy = geoms[i]
        m_loc = g['mask']
        local = np.zeros(m_loc.shape, int)
        # darker surroundings (for the reflected rim): ground in shadow, pockets
        dark_loc = g['shadow'] & ~m_loc
        v = paint_rock(local, m_loc, g['L'], s['rock'], N=g['N'], dark_nb=dark_loc, seed=s.get('seed', i),
                       P=(P or {}).get('stone'))
        V = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), int)
        h, w = m_loc.shape
        y0, x0 = max(0, oy), max(0, ox)
        y1, x1 = min(cv.H, oy + h), min(cv.W, ox + w)
        V[y0:y1, x0:x1] = local[y0 - oy:y1 - oy, x0 - ox:x1 - ox]
        sel = masks[i]
        cv.step[sel] = V[sel]
        cv.mat[sel] = ROCK_IDS[s['rock']]
        cv.wet[sel] = 1 if s.get('wet') else 0
        cv.zid[sel] = i
        s['_N'] = (g['N'], ox, oy)
    # ---- cast shadows on neighbours: stone j is shadowed by i if i is nearer the sun
    #      (larger projection on the sun direction in the ground plane) and i's footprint covers it
    cen = {i: np.array([stones[i]['x'], -stones[i]['y'] * 1.4]) for i in order}  # screen up = away
    for j in order:
        sh_j = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), bool)
        for i in order:
            if i == j:
                continue
            if (cen[i] - cen[j]) @ sund > 0:          # i sits sunward of j
                sh_j |= shads[i] & ~masks[i]
        sel = vis[j] & sh_j
        if sel.any():
            # repaint in the shadow family, keeping a hint of form (lighter toward the bottom: bounce)
            cur = cv.step[sel]
            cv.step[sel] = np.where(cur >= FL, SH_LIGHT, np.where(cur >= TURN, SH, SH_DARK))
        cv.shadow |= sh_j & vis[j]
    # ---- crevices: a farther stone's pixels touching a nearer stone's silhouette drop to a pocket
    for i in order:
        for j in order:
            if stones[j]['y'] >= stones[i]['y']:
                continue                                       # j must be farther than i
            touch = vis[j] & ndi.binary_dilation(vis[i], CROSS)
            touch2 = vis[j] & ndi.binary_dilation(vis[i], CROSS, iterations=2) & ~touch
            cv.step[touch] = np.minimum(cv.step[touch], SH_DARK)
            cv.step[touch2] = np.minimum(cv.step[touch2], np.maximum(cv.step[touch2] - 2, SH))
    # ---- ground: shadows and pockets
    ground = ~anyst
    allsh = np.zeros((cv.H, cv.W), bool)
    for i in order:
        allsh |= shads[i]
    cv.shadow |= allsh & ground
    return dict(masks=masks, vis=vis, shads=shads, anyst=anyst, ground=ground, order=order)


def paint_ground_gravel(cv, info, rng, lit_x=1.0, pocket=0.9, fall=2.0, grain=0.18):
    """the gravel between cobbles: lit TURN with sparse pebble ticks; shadow SH_LIGHT/SH; pockets."""
    ground = info['ground']
    anyst = info['anyst']
    crowd = np.zeros_like(ground)
    for k in (1, 2):
        crowd |= shift(anyst, 0, k)
    dist = ndi.distance_transform_edt(~(crowd | anyst))
    H, W = ground.shape
    # lit: TURN with FD pebble tops and EDGE undersides (a pebble = light over dark pixel pair)
    lit = np.full((H, W), TURN)
    r = rng.random((H, W))
    peb = (r < grain) & ~shift(r < grain, 0, 1) & ~shift(r < grain, 1, 0)
    lit = np.where(peb, FD, lit)
    lit = np.where(shift(peb, 0, 1) & ~peb, EDGE, lit)
    sh = np.where(peb, SH_LIGHT, SH)
    sh = np.where(shift(peb, 0, 1) & ~peb, SH_DARK, sh)
    x = np.clip((dist - pocket) / fall, 0, 1)
    pk = ordered(x * 1.0, (CONTACT, -1))
    base = np.where(cv.shadow, sh, lit)
    base = np.where(pk == CONTACT, CONTACT, base)
    cv.step[ground] = base[ground]

def water_ramp(hour='noon', silt=0.35):
    """glacial water: the sky's hue, value-compressed, with a milky silt tint (glacial flour)
    pulling the darks toward teal-grey.  6 steps: 0 deep/under-bank dark .. 5 sky glint."""
    w = WATER[hour]
    out = []
    for i, L in enumerate(w['L']):
        ab = hue(*w['sky']) * (0.5 + 0.1 * i) * (1 - silt) + hue(200, 10) * silt
        out.append(lab_to_rgb(np.array([L, *ab])))
    return [tuple(int(c) for c in x) for x in out]

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/cobbles; cat > water.py <<'EOF'
"""water.py -- stones at the waterline (stage 4b / 6): submerged parts, the lapping line,
wet darkening with a sharp glint, and the reflection.

Rules (Ferrari c_stones / master-copy C4 / analysis 1.9, and the Kuban + Hope photos):

  * the water surface is horizontal ROWS: a base step, sparse 1-row dash lines one step lighter;
  * a stone meets the water in a FLAT run; right under it a 1-px LAP line of light (the film of
    water climbing the stone catches the sky), broken where ripples cross;
  * the stone is WET in a band above the waterline (darker, more saturated ramp) and its lit
    side carries a 1-3 px glint (near white) -- the only pure light on a wet stone;
  * the submerged part is seen through the water: a darker ghost of the stone, 1-2 steps
    above the water's darkest, fading with depth;
  * the reflection is an exact mirror, value-compressed (lights drop, darks stay), broken into
    1-row streaks with gaps, shorter than the stone (you see the underside).
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from stone import shift, edge_dist_down, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP

W_DEEP, W_DARK, W_MID, W_BASE, W_LIGHT, W_GLINT = range(6)


def apply_water(cv, info, yw, rng, wet_rows=3, depth_fade=4, dash=0.06, reflect=True):
    """yw: array (W,) water-surface row per column; pixels with y > yw are water."""
    H, W = cv.H, cv.W
    yy = np.arange(H)[:, None]
    water = yy > yw[None, :]
    stone = info['anyst']
    step = cv.step.copy()
    # ---- wet band on stones above the waterline
    wetband = stone & ~water & (yy > yw[None, :] - wet_rows)
    cv.wet[wetband] = 1
    # a wet stone's glint: its brightest lit pixels near the top of the wet part become TOP
    for i in info['order']:
        m = info['vis'][i] & ~water
        if (m & wetband).any() and (m & (step >= FD)).any():
            cv.wet[m] = 1                   # the whole visible stone is wet at the edge of the water
            lit = m & (step >= FL)
            if lit.sum() >= 2:
                ys, xs = np.nonzero(lit)
                k = np.argmin(ys + 0.3 * xs)          # highest, toward the light (left)
                g = np.zeros_like(m); g[ys[k], xs[k]] = True
                if lit.sum() > 12:
                    g |= shift(g, 1, 0) & lit
                cv.step[g] = TOP
    # ---- water body
    wv = np.full((H, W), W_BASE)
    rows = rng.random(H) < 0.35
    dashes = (rng.random((H, W)) < dash) & rows[:, None]
    dashes = dashes | (shift(dashes, 1, 0) & (rng.random((H, W)) < 0.7))
    wv = np.where(dashes, W_LIGHT, wv)
    # submerged stone ghosts
    sub = stone & water
    depth = yy - yw[None, :]
    ghost = np.where(step >= FD, W_MID, W_DARK)
    ghost = np.where(depth > depth_fade, np.minimum(ghost + 1, W_BASE), ghost)
    wv = np.where(sub, ghost, wv)
    # reflection of what stands above the water (mirror about the surface row)
    if reflect:
        src = (2 * yw[None, :] - yy).astype(int)
        ok = water & (src >= 0) & (src < H)
        srcc = np.clip(src, 0, H - 1)
        xs = np.broadcast_to(np.arange(W)[None, :], (H, W))
        refl_st = stone[srcc, xs] & ok & ~sub
        sv = step[srcc, xs]
        rv = np.where(sv >= FL, W_MID, np.where(sv >= EDGE, W_DARK, W_DEEP))
        cut = (rng.random(H) < 0.3)[:, None] | (rng.random((H, W)) < 0.08)
        refl = refl_st & ~cut & (depth <= 0.7 * (yw[None, :] - np.where(stone, yy, H)).clip(0) + 99)
        wv = np.where(refl, rv, wv)
    # ---- lap line: the first water row under a stone that stands in it
    stand = stone & ~water
    lap = water & shift(stand, 0, 1) & ~sub
    lap_broken = lap & (rng.random((H, W)) > 0.15)
    wv = np.where(lap_broken, W_GLINT, wv)
    # dark film just below the lap (the stone's shadow in the water)
    under = water & shift(lap, 0, 1) & ~sub
    wv = np.where(under, np.minimum(wv, W_DARK), wv)
    # stone pixels on the surface row: the wet contact run is the darkest stone step
    cont = stand & shift(water, 0, -1)
    cv.step[cont] = np.minimum(cv.step[cont], SH_DARK)
    cv.water = water & ~(stand)
    cv.step[cv.water] = wv[cv.water]
    return cv
EOF

def waterline(hour='noon', sun=(140, 40), W=112, H=62, seed=0):
    from scene import Canvas, place, paint_ground_gravel
    from water import apply_water
    from palette import water_ramp
    stones = [dict(x=24, y=34, D=30, rock='granite', seed=3), dict(x=58, y=38, D=34, rock='greywacke', seed=4),
              dict(x=90, y=33, D=22, rock='basalt', seed=5), dict(x=40, y=44, D=14, rock='basalt', seed=6),
              dict(x=76, y=46, D=12, rock='granite', seed=7), dict(x=104, y=44, D=10, rock='greywacke', seed=8)]
    cv = Canvas(W, H)
    info = place(cv, stones, sun=sun)
    paint_ground_gravel(cv, info, np.random.default_rng(seed))
    yw = np.full(W, 36) + (np.arange(W) // 37) % 2
    apply_water(cv, info, yw, np.random.default_rng(seed + 1))
    return cv.render(hour, water_pal=water_ramp(hour))


if __name__ == '__main__' and len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == 'wet':

    b = waterline('golden', (160, 18))

Write: elements/cobbles/bar.py
"""bar.py -- a gravel bar surface: many cobbles as one mass (stage 5), its edges (stage 6).

Size in pixels = world size (m) x scale(row) (scale grows toward the viewer).  World size is
sorted along the bar: coarse at the HEAD (upstream end), fine at the TAIL.  Each stone falls
into one of three mark classes by its pixel size -- the hand-off between them is the lesson:

   D >= 5 px    a STONE: full stone.py painter (face, crescent, contact, crevices, shadows)
   2 <= D < 5   a TICK: a mini-lozenge stamp -- light top row, dark bottom row, 45 deg ends
                (Ferrari's shore stones), lit/dark by the sun side; no dither
   D < 2        GRAIN: the stones are below a pixel; the surface is a stipple of 2-3 adjacent
                steps whose light-pixel share carries the lit/shadow mix and whose contrast
                falls with distance (farther = closer steps, finally flat)

Stones are packed back to front on a jittered grid so they touch and overlap (a bar is a
clast-supported pile, not stones sprinkled on sand); the gaps show the finer matrix.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from stone import ordered, shift, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP
from scene import Canvas, place, paint_ground_gravel, ROCK_IDS

ROCK_MIX = [('greywacke', 0.55), ('granite', 0.25), ('basalt', 0.2)]


def pick_rock(rng, mix=ROCK_MIX):
    r = rng.random()
    acc = 0
    for k, p in mix:
        acc += p
        if r < acc:
            return k
    return mix[-1][0]


def tick_stamp(cv, x, y, D, rock, sun_left, rng):
    """a 2-4 px stone: rows [light top] [mid/dark body] [dark bottom], ends clipped at 45 deg."""
    w = max(2, int(round(D)))
    h = max(1, int(round(D * 0.6)))
    x0 = int(round(x - w / 2)); y0 = int(round(y - h))
    for r in range(h + 1):
        for c in range(w):
            if (r == 0 or r == h) and (c == 0 or c == w - 1) and w >= 3:
                continue                                 # 45-degree ends
            X, Y = x0 + c, y0 + r
            if not (0 <= X < cv.W and 0 <= Y < cv.H):
                continue
            if r == 0:
                st = FL
            elif r == h:
                st = CONTACT if rng.random() < 0.7 else SH_DARK   # the pocket under the tick
                cv.step[Y, X] = st; cv.mat[Y, X] = -1
                continue
            else:
                st = FD if r < h - 1 else EDGE
            # the side away from the sun turns
            if (c == w - 1 and sun_left) or (c == 0 and not sun_left):
                st = min(st, TURN) if r > 0 else FD
            cv.step[Y, X] = st
            cv.mat[Y, X] = ROCK_IDS[rock]


def grain_fill(cv, region, contrast, lit_share, rng, shadow=None):
    """sub-pixel gravel: a stipple of adjacent steps.  contrast in 0..1 (1 = near), lit_share =
    fraction of light pixels.  In cast shadow the same stipple on the shadow family."""
    H, W = cv.H, cv.W
    r = rng.random((H, W))
    # clumpy (2x1 pebbles read better than white noise): light pixel with a dark one below
    light = r < lit_share
    dark = shift(light, 0, 1) & ~light & (rng.random((H, W)) < contrast)
    mid = TURN
    lo = np.where(contrast > 0.5, EDGE, TURN)
    hi = np.where(contrast > 0.35, FD, TURN)
    st = np.where(light, hi, np.where(dark, lo, mid))
    if shadow is not None:
        st_sh = np.where(light, SH_LIGHT, np.where(dark, SH_DARK, SH))
        st = np.where(shadow, st_sh, st)
    cv.step[region] = st[region]
    cv.mat[region] = -1


def bar(W=240, H=140, sun=(140, 40), hour='noon', seed=0, head_m=0.25, tail_m=0.04,
        px_per_m_far=18.0, px_per_m_near=150.0, density=1.0, horizon=0):
    """paint a bar surface filling the canvas.  Rows near the top are far."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    cv = Canvas(W, H)
    scale = lambda y: px_per_m_far + (px_per_m_near - px_per_m_far) * (max(y, 0) / H) ** 1.3
    stones, ticks = [], []
    y = horizon + 1.0
    while y < H + 6:
        s_row = scale(y)
        x = rng.uniform(-4, 2)
        row_max = 0
        while x < W + 4:
            t = np.clip(x / W, 0, 1)
            size_m = np.exp(np.log(head_m) * (1 - t) + np.log(tail_m) * t) * np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.45))
            D = size_m * s_row
            if D >= 5:
                stones.append(dict(x=x + D / 2, y=y + rng.uniform(-0.3, 0.3) * D * 0.4, D=D, rock=pick_rock(rng),
                                   seed=int(rng.integers(1e6)), yaw=rng.uniform(-40, 40),
                                   elong=rng.uniform(1.15, 1.6), flat=rng.uniform(0.5, 0.75)))
            elif D >= 2:
                ticks.append((x + D / 2, y, D))
            x += max(1.5, D * rng.uniform(0.75, 1.05) / density)
            row_max = max(row_max, D)
        # rows advance by the typical stone height at this depth
        typ = np.exp(np.log(head_m) * 0.5 + np.log(tail_m) * 0.5) * s_row
        y += max(1.0, 0.45 * min(typ, 40))
    # --- grain everywhere first (the matrix), then ticks, then real stones on top
    region = np.ones((H, W), bool)
    yy = np.arange(H)[:, None] * np.ones((1, W))
    contrast = np.clip((scale(yy) - px_per_m_far) / (60.0), 0.15, 1)
    grain_fill(cv, region, contrast, 0.3, rng)
    sun_left = 90 < sun[0] < 270
    for (x, y, D) in ticks:
        tick_stamp(cv, x, y, D, pick_rock(rng), sun_left, rng)
    stones = [s for s in stones if -20 < s['x'] < W + 20 and s['y'] < H + 20]
    info = None
    if stones:
        base_step, base_mat = cv.step.copy(), cv.mat.copy()
        info = place(cv, stones, sun=sun)
        # restore the matrix/ticks where no stone landed, then add contact/gap pockets
        g = info['ground']
        cv.step[g] = base_step[g]; cv.mat[g] = base_mat[g]
        pockets(cv, info)
    return cv, info


def pockets(cv, info, gap_px=2.0):
    g = info['ground']
    from stone import edge_dist_down
    anyst = info['anyst']
    # contact under stones and dark wedges in narrow gaps
    for i in info['order']:
        m = info['vis'][i]
        e = edge_dist_down(m)
        below = shift(m & (e == 0), 0, 1) & g
        cv.step[below] = CONTACT
    close = ndi.binary_closing(anyst, np.ones((3, 3), bool), iterations=1) & g
    cv.step[close] = np.minimum(cv.step[close], SH_DARK)
    # cast shadow on the matrix
    sh = cv.shadow & g
    cv.step[sh] = np.where(cv.step[sh] >= FD, SH_LIGHT, np.where(cv.step[sh] >= TURN, SH, np.minimum(cv.step[sh], SH_DARK)))

## Round 3 — 2026-09-25 21:46 — stages 2–4: `img/stage2_materials.png`, `stage3_colour_hours.png`, `stage4_nestled.png`, `stage4_waterline.png`
**REVISE on all of them.** You've gone from stage 1 to stage 4 in one round, and stages 0 and 1 are still carrying their round-2 problems. Every later stage inherits them. (Your call, but I'd go back.)

What's working:

- **Stage 3 colour.** The three materials are distinguishable by hue and value alone (pink-grey granite, olive greywacke with a quartz vein, blue-black basalt). The hour palettes shift the lit colour and cool the shadows sensibly. Golden and dusk have the right warmth, and the shadow family stays separate from the lit family.
- **Stage 4 waterline.** The dead-flat waterline with a lighter lip is correct Ferrari grammar.

What's wrong, biggest first:

1. **Every stone is still the same perfect ellipse** (stages 2, 3, 4), just scaled. In `stage4_nestled.png` that makes the group read as eggs on a tray. River cobbles in `refs/close/commons_Hope_river_cobbles_*` and `commons_Cobbles_in_Kuban_River_*` are flattened, lopsided discs, rounded triangles and loaves, with flat tops and straight-ish undersides. This was the round-2 note and it's now the most visible problem at every stage.
2. **"Nestled" isn't nestled.** The stones sit apart on a sand field with gaps between them. In your refs, cobbles pack edge to edge, overlap, and are half-buried, with small gravel and dark pockets filling the joints. The mass reads through overlap and contact shadows between stones, not through cast shadows on open sand. Ferrari's V19 pile (your stage 0) is exactly this: stones lapping stones, black pockets where they meet.
3. **Cast shadows fall as ruled polygons across other stones.** In "noon" the pink stone throws a flat dark strip with straight edges across the middle green stone; in "golden" long shadows cross two or three stones as one flat-tone band with ruled diagonal edges. A shadow falling across a curved stone bends with its surface and takes that stone's own shadow colour.
4. **The ground is random speckle** (stage 4): isotropic noise at one scale, which reads as sandpaper. Fine gravel between cobbles has small stones with light tops and dark bottoms (the SYNTHESIS "stone" mark at 2–3 px).
5. **Waterline reflections** (`stage4_waterline.png`). The reflection is a blue-grey checker blob that doesn't mirror the stones' shapes or colours. Ferrari's reflection (SYNTHESIS, master copy C4) is an exact mirror with a compressed range: lights drop, darks stay, the tint goes slightly cool, and there are horizontal dash rows. There's also a stray white 2 px block on the left stone (≈x=130, y=170 in both panels) and a black notch under the upper-left stone.

### Stage 1
The staging is good: a flat ground, a cast shadow on a single tone, a 1 px contact line, and a size ladder. The small sizes from 6 px down read as pebbles.

What's wrong:

- **Outline: every stone is a perfect ellipse.** Look at your own photos in `stage1_compare.png`. The Kuban stone has a flattened top plane, one fatter end, and a nearly straight underside; the Hope stone is a rounded triangle. Ferrari's boulder has a faceted contour: short straight runs meeting at soft corners. A perfect ellipse is the iconic "egg" and the first thing a painter's eye rejects.
- **The terminator is a clean S-curve** ("sun behind-left", "sun low left"). It reads as a moon phase. His terminator (V19) is broken by planes, with a step where one facet turns away.
- **The lit face is a perforated grid.** At 40 px the light side is evenly spaced single dots of the lighter tone on a regular lattice, like pegboard. In his boulder the checker is dense in the core and sparse to absent on the brightest plane. Your stage 0 learned this; carry it over.
- **The 10 px stone** in every row reads as a little hat or box (light cap, hard black base). The 6 px and 4 px stones are better.
- No reflected light: the shadow side is one flat dark with checker. On sunny gravel the ground bounces light into the lower shadow side (see the Kuban photo's lower edge). Try one step lighter just above the contact line.

a = D / 2.0
    b = a / elong
    c = a * flat
    th = np.linspace(-np.pi / 2, np.pi / 2, res)[:, None]      # latitude
    ph = np.linspace(-np.pi, np.pi, 2 * res, endpoint=False)[None, :]
    e = 2.0 / n

    def sgnpow(x, p):
        return np.sign(x) * np.abs(x) ** p
    X = a * sgnpow(np.cos(th), e) * sgnpow(np.cos(ph), e)
    Y = b * sgnpow(np.cos(th), e) * sgnpow(np.sin(ph), e)
    Z = c * sgnpow(np.sin(th), e) * np.ones_like(ph)
    # lumps: a few low-order bumps in direction space (water-worn: broad, never sharp)
    dirs = np.stack([np.cos(th) * np.cos(ph), np.cos(th) * np.sin(ph), np.sin(th) * np.ones_like(ph)], -1)
    k = np.ones(dirs.shape[:2])
    for _ in range(5):
        v = unit(rng.normal(size=3))
        k += lump * rng.normal() * np.exp((dirs @ v - 1) * 2.5)
    # flatten the base a little (cobbles rest on a flat-ish face)
    P = np.stack([X * k, Y * k, Z * k], -1)
    P[..., 2] = np.where(P[..., 2] < 0, P[..., 2] * 0.8, P[..., 2])
    # normals by finite differences on the parameter grid
    dth = np.gradient(P, axis=0)
    dph = np.roll(P, -1, axis=1) - np.roll(P, 1, axis=1)
    N = np.cross(dph, dth)
    nn = np.linalg.norm(N, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
    N = N / np.maximum(nn, 1e-9)
    # orient outward
    out = np.sum(N * P, -1) < 0
    N[out] *= -1
    # poles: normals degenerate -> use position direction
    bad = nn[..., 0] < 1e-6
    N[bad] = unit_rows(P[bad] * [1 / a ** 2, 1 / b ** 2, 1 / c ** 2]) if bad.any() else N[bad]
    yaw = np.radians(yaw_deg)
    R = np.array([[np.cos(yaw), -np.sin(yaw), 0], [np.sin(yaw), np.cos(yaw), 0], [0, 0, 1]])
    P = P @ R.T
    N = N @ R.T
    zmin = P[..., 2].min()
    P[..., 2] -= zmin + sink * (P[..., 2].max() - zmin)
    return P.reshape(-1, 3), N.reshape(-1, 3), c * 2 * (1 - sink)


def unit_rows(V):
    return V / np.maximum(np.linalg.norm(V, axis=-1, keepdims=True), 1e-9)

s=open('materials.py').read()
a=s.index('def greywacke_veins'); b=s.index('def basalt_marks')
new='''def wander_line(m, rng):
    """a vein: 2-4 segments, each a clean ratio, joined end to end (it wanders, but every
    segment is a clean pixel run), crossing the whole stone."""
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(m)
    y = ys.mean() + rng.normal() * np.ptp(ys) * 0.2
    x = xs.min() - 1.0
    ang0 = rng.uniform(-0.6, 0.6)
    line = np.zeros_like(m)
    choices = [0.0, 0.25, 1 / 3, 0.5, 1.0]
    while x < xs.max() + 1:
        seg = rng.integers(3, 8)
        slope = choices[rng.integers(len(choices))] * (1 if ang0 + rng.normal() * 0.4 > 0 else -1)
        for _ in range(seg):
            x += 1; y += slope
            yi, xi = int(round(y)), int(round(x))
            if 0 <= yi < m.shape[0] and 0 <= xi < m.shape[1]:
                line[yi, xi] = True
    return line & m


def greywacke_veins(v, m, rng, size, n=None, lit=None):
    if size < 10:
        return v
    n = n if n is not None else rng.choice([0, 1, 1, 2])
    for _ in range(n):
        ln = wander_line(m, rng) & (edge_dist_down(m) > 0)
        # brighter in the light, one step in shadow, and broken here and there (the vein dives)
        gap = rng.random(m.shape) < 0.12
        k = np.where(lit if lit is not None else True, 2, 1)
        v = np.where(ln & ~gap, step_up(v, 1) * 0 + np.where(k == 2, step_up(v, 2), step_up(v, 1)), v)
    return v


'''

            wv = np.where(r_st & (depth == 2) & ~sub, W_DARK, wv)""","""            # an exact mirror in the stone's own ramp, range compressed: lights drop two steps,
            # darks stay; broken by 1-row streaks of the water's lighter dash colour
            comp = np.where(sv >= EDGE, np.maximum(sv - 2, SH_LIGHT), sv)
            comp = np.where(depth == 2, np.minimum(comp, SH), comp)     # the underside, darkest
            cut = (rng.random(H) < 0.2)[:, None] | (rng.random((H, W)) < 0.06)
            sel = r_st & ~cut & ~sub
            refl_px |= sel
            refl_step = np.where(sel, comp, refl_step)
            refl_mat = np.where(sel, mat0[srcc, xsg], refl_mat)""")
s=s.replace("""    cv.water = water
    cv.step[water] = wv[water]
    cv.mat[water] = -1
    return cv""","""    cv.water = water & ~refl_px
    cv.step[cv.water] = wv[cv.water]
    cv.mat[cv.water] = -1
    cv.step[refl_px] = refl_step[refl_px]
    cv.mat[refl_px] = refl_mat[refl_px]
    cv.wet[refl_px] = 1
    return cv""")
open('water.py','w').write(s)

def grass_ramp(hour='noon'):
    """bank grass / sedge: olive shadow -> ochre light (Ferrari's V16 lawn family). 6 steps."""
    h = HOUR[hour]
    Ls = [18, 28, 38, 50, 62, 74]

cat > edges.py <<'EOF'
"""edges.py -- where the bar meets water, and where it meets grass or scrub (stage 6).

WATER EDGE (bar side): the bar slopes into the channel, so the edge is a sequence of bands:
  dry bar -> damp band (the stones darker, the matrix darker: capillary wetting, 2-4 rows)
  -> the waterline (dead flat per run, stepping in clean runs where the bar lobes) with a
  broken 1-px LAP of light hugging every stone that stands in it -> shallows (bed gravel seen
  through the water, fading into the silt colour) -> open water rows with dashes.
GRASS EDGE: never a line.  The mass of grass ends in a FRINGE: the gravel runs under it, tufts
  of 3-6 blades fan from one foot and break upward over the stones, single tufts colonise the
  gravel for a few rows beyond the edge (sparser with distance from it), the ground right
  under the grass is dark (the sod shades it), and the blades are paired strokes
  [dark gap][lit blade][stem] leaning in a common wind direction.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from stone import shift, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD, FL, TOP

G_BLACK, G_DARK, G_OLIVE, G_MID, G_LIT, G_TIP = range(6)


def clean_edge(W, rng, base, amp, seg=(4, 12), slopes=(0, 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0)):
    """a boundary row per column made of clean runs (flat runs and clean diagonals)."""
    y = float(base); out = np.zeros(W)
    x = 0
    while x < W:
        n = int(rng.integers(*seg))
        sl = slopes[rng.integers(len(slopes))] * rng.choice([-1, 1])
        if abs(y + sl * n - base) > amp:
            sl = -np.sign(y - base) * abs(sl)
        for k in range(n):
            if x >= W:
                break
            out[x] = round(y); y += sl; x += 1
    return out.astype(int)


def blade(gstep, gmask, x, y, h, lean, lit):
    """one blade as a clean run from its foot (x,y) upward h px, leaning 1 px per `lean` rows.
    Lit blade = G_LIT with G_TIP at the tip and a G_BLACK gap on its shadow side."""
    H, W = gstep.shape
    px = x
    for k in range(h):
        yy = y - k
        if lean and k and k % lean == 0:
            px += np.sign(lean)
        if 0 <= yy < H and 0 <= px < W:
            gstep[yy, px] = (G_TIP if k == h - 1 else G_LIT) if lit else (G_MID if k > h // 2 else G_OLIVE)
            gmask[yy, px] = True
            gx = px + (1 if lit else -1)
            if 0 <= gx < W and not gmask[yy, gx] and k < h - 1:
                gstep[yy, gx] = G_BLACK if lit else G_DARK
                gmask[yy, gx] = True


def tuft(gstep, gmask, x, y, rng, hmax, wind=1):
    n = int(rng.integers(3, 7))
    for i in range(n):
        off = int(round((i - n / 2) * 0.8))
        h = max(2, int(hmax * (1 - abs(i - n / 2) / (n + 1)) * rng.uniform(0.7, 1.1)))
        lean = int(rng.choice([0, 3, 2, 4])) * (wind if off >= 0 else -wind if rng.random() < 0.5 else wind)
        blade(gstep, gmask, x + off, y, h, lean, lit=rng.random() < 0.55)


def grass_band(W, H, edge, rng, hmax=7, colonise=6):
    """grass above `edge` (row per column): a dense lawn of strokes + a fringe breaking below."""
    gstep = np.full((H, W), -1, int)
    gmask = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    yy = np.arange(H)[:, None]
    body = yy < edge[None, :]
    # the sod body: olive strokes, vertical, with lit ones scattered (paired)
    base = np.where(rng.random((H, W)) < 0.35, G_MID, G_OLIVE)
    gstep[body] = base[body]
    gmask |= body
    for y in range(0, H, 2):
        for x in range(W):
            if y < edge[x] - 1 and rng.random() < 0.12:
                blade(gstep, gmask, x, y + 3, int(rng.integers(3, hmax)), 0, lit=rng.random() < 0.5)
    # fringe: tufts rooted along the edge, breaking over the gravel
    x = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
    while x < W:
        tuft(gstep, gmask, x, edge[x] + int(rng.integers(0, 3)), rng, hmax)
        x += int(rng.integers(2, 5))
    # colonising tufts in the gravel, thinning away from the edge
    for _ in range(int(W * colonise / 10)):
        x = int(rng.integers(0, W))
        d = int(abs(rng.exponential(colonise / 2))) + 2
        tuft(gstep, gmask, x, edge[x] + d, rng, max(3, hmax - d // 2))
    return gstep, gmask
EOF

"""stage6.py -- the bar's edges: into water, and into grass."""
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, __file__.rsplit('/', 1)[0])
import numpy as np
from bar import bar
from water import apply_water
from edges import clean_edge, grass_band
from palette import water_ramp, grass_ramp
from stone import shift, CONTACT, SH_DARK, SH, SH_LIGHT, EDGE, TURN, FD
from util import *


def scene(hour='noon', sun=(140, 55), seed=3, W=200, H=120):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    cv, info = bar(W=W, H=H, sun=sun, seed=seed, head_m=0.14, tail_m=0.05, px_per_m_far=40,
                   px_per_m_near=110)
    # --- water at the bottom: a lobed waterline in clean runs
    yw = clean_edge(W, rng, base=int(H * 0.72), amp=5)
    # damp band above the waterline: darker matrix and wet stones
    yy = np.arange(H)[:, None]
    damp = (yy > yw[None, :] - 4) & (yy <= yw[None, :])
    g = info['ground']
    cv.step[damp & g] = np.maximum(cv.step[damp & g] - 1, CONTACT)
    cv.wet[damp & ~g] = 1
    apply_water(cv, info, yw, np.random.default_rng(seed + 1), shallow=6)
    img = cv.render(hour, water_pal=water_ramp(hour))
    # --- grass at the top
    edge = clean_edge(W, rng, base=int(H * 0.2), amp=6)
    gs, gm = grass_band(W, H, edge, rng)
    # the ground just under the sod is shaded
    shade = (yy >= edge[None, :]) & (yy < edge[None, :] + 2) & ~gm
    gp = np.array(grass_ramp(hour), np.uint8)
    from palette import ground_ramp
    gr = np.array(ground_ramp(hour), np.uint8)
    img[shade] = gr[SH_DARK]
    img[gm] = gp[gs[gm]]
    return img


if __name__ == '__main__':
    a = scene()
    save(label(up(a, 4), 'bar edges: grass fringe (top), damp band + waterline + shallows (bottom). noon'), 'stage6_edges.png')
    save(label(up(a[10:50, 20:120], 8), 'grass fringe 8x'), 'stage6_grass_zoom.png')
    save(label(up(a[70:110, 20:120], 8), 'water edge 8x'), 'stage6_water_zoom.png')

cat > cobbles_api.py <<'EOF'
"""cobbles_api.py -- the technique as one call with parameters (stage 7).

paint_patch(W, H, rock, size_m, wet, density, sun, hour, px_per_stone, seed) -> RGB uint8

  rock          'granite' | 'greywacke' | 'basalt' | 'mixed' (a braided-river bar: mostly
                greywacke, some granite, some basalt)
  size_m        median clast size in metres (sets the lognormal spread's centre)
  px_per_stone  the median stone's size in PIXELS -- the distance knob.  >= 5: stones;
                2-5: ticks; < 2: grain
  wet           0 dry .. 1 all wet (stones on the wet ramp: darker, saturated, glints)
  density       packing: 1 = clast-supported (touching), < 1 opens matrix between
  sun           (azimuth, elevation) in degrees; hour picks the palette (the light's colour)
  seed          everything random
"""
import numpy as np
from bar import bar
from palette import water_ramp

MIXES = {'granite': [('granite', 1.0)], 'greywacke': [('greywacke', 1.0)], 'basalt': [('basalt', 1.0)],
         'mixed': [('greywacke', 0.55), ('granite', 0.25), ('basalt', 0.2)]}
HOUR_SUN = {'noon': (120, 62), 'morning': (30, 30), 'golden': (160, 16), 'dusk': (175, 7),
            'overcast': (100, 80)}


def paint_patch(W=96, H=64, rock='mixed', size_m=0.12, wet=0.0, density=1.0, sun=None, hour='noon',
                px_per_stone=16, seed=0, depth_ratio=1.0):
    sun = sun or HOUR_SUN[hour]
    ppm = px_per_stone / size_m
    cv, info = bar(W=W, H=H, sun=sun, seed=seed, head_m=size_m, tail_m=size_m,
                   px_per_m_far=ppm / depth_ratio, px_per_m_near=ppm, density=density, mix=MIXES[rock],
                   size_sd=0.4)
    if wet > 0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 7)
        st = cv.mat >= 0
        cv.wet[st & (rng.random(cv.step.shape) < 1.0)] = 1 if wet >= 0.5 else 0
        if wet < 0.5:
            # damp: only the lower half of the patch
            yy = np.arange(H)[:, None] * np.ones((1, W))
            cv.wet[st & (yy > H * (1 - 2 * wet))] = 1
        g = cv.mat < 0
        cv.step[g] = np.maximum(cv.step[g] - int(round(wet * 2)), 0)
    return cv.render(hour)
EOF
cat > stage7.py <<'EOF'
"""stage7.py -- labelled parameter sheets of the cobble technique."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, __file__.rsplit('/', 1)[0])
from multiprocessing import Pool
import numpy as np
from cobbles_api import paint_patch
from util import *

CELL = dict(W=96, H=64)


def job(kw):
    return paint_patch(**{**CELL, **kw})


def sheet(rows, cols, make, name, title_txt, s=3):
    jobs = [make(r, c) for r in rows for c in cols]
    with Pool(4) as p:
        imgs = p.map(job, jobs)
    out = []
    k = 0
    for r in rows:
        cells = []
        for c in cols:
            cells.append(label(up(imgs[k], s), f'{c}', sz=12)); k += 1
        out.append(label(hcat(cells), f'{r}', sz=14))
    save(title(vcat(out, gap=8), title_txt), name)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    which = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'all'
    if which in ('A', 'all'):
        sheet(['noon', 'morning', 'golden', 'dusk', 'overcast'], [40, 20, 10, 5, 2.5],
              lambda r, c: dict(hour=r, px_per_stone=c, rock='mixed', seed=11),
              'sheet_A_hour_x_distance.png', 'Sheet A: hour (rows) x px per median stone (cols); mixed rock, dry, seed 11')
    if which in ('B', 'all'):
        sheet(['granite', 'greywacke', 'basalt', 'mixed'], ['dry', 'damp', 'wet'],
              lambda r, c: dict(rock=r, wet={'dry': 0, 'damp': 0.3, 'wet': 1}[c], px_per_stone=16, seed=5),
              'sheet_B_rock_x_wetness.png', 'Sheet B: rock (rows) x wetness (cols); 16 px/stone, noon')
    if which in ('C', 'all'):
        sheet(['density 1.2', 'density 1.0', 'density 0.7', 'density 0.45'], [1, 2, 3],
              lambda r, c: dict(density=float(r.split()[1]), seed=c, px_per_stone=14),
              'sheet_C_density_x_seed.png', 'Sheet C: packing density (rows) x seed (cols); 14 px/stone, noon')

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 5 — 2026-09-25 22:12 — stage 6 `img/stage6_edges.png`, `stage6_water_zoom.png`, `stage6_grass_zoom.png`; sheet `img/sheet_A_hour_x_distance.png`
**REVISE.** You're now on the sheet stage. Progress since round 4: the veins wander and break (sheet A, 40 and 20 columns), which fixes the ruled veins. But the main round-4 note didn't get addressed, and it's now copied into every cell of the sheet. (My read; your call.)

1. **Leaded glass, still.** Sheet A columns 20 and 10, every hour: every stone has a continuous dark ring, including lit-against-lit edges. It's most visible in "overcast 10" and "noon 20", where the bar reads as a honeycomb of cells. This is the single thing standing between the sheet and Ferrari's V19. Let the dark come only in pockets and on the shadow side of the contacts.
2. **Dark crescent hooks at 40 px** (sheet A, "noon 40", "golden 40", "dusk 40"): thick dark arcs cross the big stones like C-shaped bands, reading as grooves cut into the stone. If they're meant as contact shadows from the neighbours, they need to sit *at* the contact and fade, not float mid-face.
3. **The waterline is Morse code** (`stage6_water_zoom.png`): a dashed light-blue line of evenly alternating runs across the full width. That's exactly the single-row-checker failure the channel student documented four times (their LESSONS, stage 1, item 4). A waterline lap is a *continuous* light run, broken only where a stone breaks the surface.
4. **The big stone's vein is a dotted line** (water zoom, x≈40–240, y≈60–150): evenly spaced single pale pixels on a diagonal. On a stone that size a vein is a clean 1 px run, or a run with one or two breaks.
5. **The grass fringe is barcode** (`stage6_grass_zoom.png`): evenly spaced vertical ochre strands of similar height over a flat olive field, which reads as hay or rain. Ferrari's grass (SYNTHESIS: the paired blade stroke) is a lit blade with a dark gap beside it, heights varying, with tips breaking the top edge in clusters. Also, where the grass meets the stones, the stones need to overlap the grass foot and the grass needs to overlap the stones in places, so it isn't a horizontal seam.
6. **Reflections** (`stage6_edges.png`): only the big stone reflects, as dark dashes. The row of stones along the waterline to the right throws no reflection at all.

What's working: the damp band (darker stones near the water), the 5 and 2.5 px columns of sheet A, which read as coarse and fine gravel at distance, and the hour palettes, which remain your strongest element.

## Round 4 — 2026-09-25 22:00 — stage 4 `img/stage4_nestled.png` (redone); stage 5 `img/stage5_bar.png`, `stage5_bar_zoom_head.png`, `stage5_bar_zoom_far.png`
**REVISE, but this is the biggest jump anyone has made today.** The packed stage 4 and the stage 5 bar are the first cobbles that look like the Hope and Kuban photos at a glance: packed, overlapping, coarse at the head and fine at the tail, in perspective. The shapes now vary

new='''    # ---- joins between stones (no leaded glass): where a nearer stone overlaps a farther one
    #  * on the shadow side of the nearer stone (its silhouette faces away from the sun) or where
    #    the farther stone is already in shadow: a dark pocket, 1 px, SH_DARK
    #  * where two lit faces meet: only a value step -- the farther pixel drops ONE step, and only
    #    if the two would otherwise be within a step of each other
    sun2 = np.array([sund[0], -sund[1]])          # screen: +y is toward the viewer (down)
    for i in order:
        di = None
        for j in order:
            if stones[j]['y'] >= stones[i]['y'] or not near(i, j, 2):
                continue
            if di is None:
                di = ndi.binary_dilation(vis[i], CROSS)
                ys_, xs_ = np.nonzero(vis[i])
                cxi, cyi = xs_.mean(), ys_.mean()
                yy_, xx_ = np.mgrid[0:cv.H, 0:cv.W]
                ox_, oy_ = xx_ - cxi, yy_ - cyi
                nrm = np.hypot(ox_, oy_) + 1e-6
                facing_sun = (ox_ * sun2[0] + oy_ * sun2[1]) / nrm > 0.15
            touch = vis[j] & di
            if not touch.any():
                continue
            fj = cv.step
            shadowy = touch & ((fj <= SH_LIGHT) | ~facing_sun)
            cv.step[shadowy] = np.minimum(cv.step[shadowy], SH_DARK)
            litj = touch & ~shadowy
            # nearer stone's value next to it
            nb = ndi.grey_dilation(np.where(vis[i], cv.step, -1), footprint=CROSS)
            close = litj & (np.abs(nb - cv.step) <= 1)
            cv.step[close] = np.maximum(cv.step[close] - 1, SH_LIGHT)
'''

# River cobbles and gravel bars, in Ferrari's language

A student's notebook, stage by stage. Every image is in `img/`. Code is in this folder;
run anything with `./run.sh <script>` (it pins BLAS to one thread, see "Tools" at the end).

**Where this stands, plainly.** Not at the master's bar. Stage 0 got the single lit boulder
close enough that the critic called its dome "hard to tell from his at 10×". The rim is still
too banded and the surrounding chips are generic. From stage 4 on, the bar reads as a
real cobble bar at 1× (packed, size-sorted, dissolving with distance, lit by an hour
palette). At 8× it's still clearly a program: cast shadows fall as flat stepped shapes,
stones at 40 px carry hooks where neighbours' shadows land, and the water is plainer than
his. Details per stage below, and the open list at the end.

---

## Stage 0: master copy (the technique comes from here)

`stage0_copy.py`. Target: Mountain Stream V19, crop (228,252) 72×48, his lit rounded
boulder in its pile. I took the *layout* as a copyist's sketch (regions from his crop,
majority-smoothed so no pixel is traced). Everything inside the regions is generated by the
procedure below. I found each rule by printing his crop as a grid of ramp ranks
(`scratch/grid.py`) and reading it row by row.

![copy](img/stage0_copy.png)
![zoom](img/stage0_boulder_zoom.png)

What his pixels say (each rule was checked against the grid):

1. **The lit face lives inside ONE dither transition.** It's a Bayer 2×2 mix of two adjacent
   light classes, from 25% light (lower left) to 75% light (upper right, toward the sun), and
   it never reaches either pure class on the face. Broad lumps make it patchy: "DDDDD" rows
   next to "GDGD" rows.
2. **The crown** (top two rows) is solid lightest.
3. **The crescent** sits opposite the light, measured in *pixels from the silhouette*. It's
   about 20% of the stone's minor size at its thickest and dies out within about 45°. The
   bands run core 8 → 8/B seam → B → B/D seam → face, and the widths jitter ±1 px.
4. **Found edge against a dark neighbour.** On the away side, the silhouette pixel carries
   reflected light (lightest high up, dimming going down) and the pixel inside it is the core
   (B high up, 8 low down). The rim is bright and the echo inside it dark.
5. **Bottom.** One row of the edge class, with CONTACT under flat runs only, never along
   diagonals.
6. **Neighbours in shadow** use their own four classes (1,3,5,6) with the same logic. They're
   lit by bounce from the lit boulder: a 6 rim along the top and on the edge facing it.
7. **Shadowed ground** is solid black pockets right under the masses, opening into a 0/5
   checker (75% 5). It is not one uniform checker; the critic caught that, and it was right.

Honest comparison: the dome is the best part. The crescent still reads as concentric bands.
In his crop the checker spills into the rim zone in staggered chunks, so there's no line
where the dome ends. The top chips are Voronoi facets, where his are drawn planes with crack
ticks. The lower-right shadow zone is too evenly checkered.

I also read (but didn't fully copy) his dusk shore stones (tarn, `analysis/img/c_stones.png`,
native (245,400)). Each is a top row one step lighter (sky-lit rim), a flat body, a 2/4
checker on the lower body, a **1-px light lap line** in the water, and reflection rows below.
Those rules became `water.py`.

---

## Stage 1: one cobble, greyscale, 40 → 4 px

`stage1.py`, `stone.py` (the painter), `cobble.py` (3D form).

![sizes](img/stage1_lights_sizes.png)
![compare](img/stage1_compare.png)

What I learned, in the order it cost me:

- **The 2D dome from stage 0 isn't a cobble.** A cobble seen from above is a *flat* stone: a
  big top plane and a narrow front band. I went back to real 3D form (`cobble_geom`): a
  lumpy flattened superellipsoid, sunk into the gravel, seen by an orthographic camera
  pitched 45°. It gives screen normals, and the stage-0 painter paints steps from those.
- **Perfect ellipses read as eggs** (critic, rounds 2 and 3, correctly). The fix went into
  the form, not the paint. Each stone is **lopsided** (one end fatter) and **cut by planes**:
  a flat top plane always, plus 4–6 side facets, with the crease softened over a few percent
  of the size. Planes give flat values, and the terminator steps at facet edges instead of
  running as a moon-phase S-curve. The silhouette gets short straight runs meeting at soft
  corners.
- **Planes, not gradients.** The face mapping is banded: flat FD and flat FL joined by a
  short checker seam. That removed the "pegboard" of evenly spaced light dots, so the
  brightest plane is flat and the checker lives where the form turns.
- **Two families.** N·L ≤ 0 goes to the shadow family (contact, core, shadow, reflected),
  with the band just above the contact one step lighter: bounce from the lit gravel.
- **Sizes.** At 9 px and above, the full painter. Below that, Ferrari's shore lozenge: three
  flat values (light top row, mid body, dark bottom row), no dither, no contact line. The 10
  px stone still reads as a little hat; that's an open item.

---

## Stage 2: material (greyscale)

`materials.py`, `stage2.py`. Marks are *offsets in step space*, in object space, always one
or two steps within the stone's own family.

![materials](img/stage2_materials.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage2.png)

- **Granite:** isolated 1-px flecks, mostly dark, gathered in patches by a noise field,
  dense in the light and rare in shadow; the lightest steps. This reads immediately.
- **Greywacke:** smooth and mid-toned, with 0–2 **quartz veins**. Each vein is 2–4 clean-run
  segments joined end to end (it wanders), two steps up in light and one in shadow, with the
  odd break. The first version was a ruled straight line, and it looked like one.
- **Basalt:** the whole family shifted a step down, a few pits, and a small glint cluster.
  In greyscale, value alone carries it.
- The biggest lesson: **at pixel scale, albedo reads before texture.** The family shift does
  most of the work, and the marks confirm it.

---

## Stage 3: colour (the palette is the light)

`palette.py`, `stage3.py`. One 9-step ramp per (rock, hour, wetness), built as a hue walk in
Lab. The shadow family takes the sky's hue and the lit family takes the sun's. The rock's
own tint (granite pink-grey, greywacke green-grey, basalt blue-black) is strongest in the
halftones, and chroma peaks there and falls toward black and the top light, as on Ferrari's
rock ramps. The lit/shadow gap is kept at the terminator.

![hours](img/stage3_colour_hours.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage3.png)

The index map doesn't change with the hour; only the ramp does. The critic calls the hour
palettes the strongest element, and I agree. Weaknesses: golden and dusk low suns throw
long, flat cast-shadow polygons. The physics is right, but they read as graphic shapes,
not painted ones.

---

## Stage 4: several cobbles nestled; a wet cobble at the waterline

`scene.py` (composition), `water.py`, `stage4.py`.

![nestled](img/stage4_nestled.png)
![waterline](img/stage4_waterline.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage4.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage4_wet.png)

- **"Nestled" means packed.** My first cluster was stones sprinkled on sand, and the critic
  was right that it read as eggs on a tray. The fix was to pack them edge to edge, overlapping
  and half-buried, back to front, with finer stones and ticks filling the joints (the same
  packer as stage 5 at a fixed scale).
- **Leaded glass** was the single biggest tell (rounds 4 and 5): every stone ringed in black.
  The joins now follow Ferrari's pocket logic. A dark 1-px pocket goes only on the *shadow
  side* of the nearer stone, or where the farther stone is already in shadow. Where two lit
  faces meet, the farther pixel drops one step, and only if the two would otherwise be within
  a step. The ground under stones is CONTACT, and narrow voids are SH_DARK. No halo.
- **Cast shadows on neighbours** now lie only within a few px of the caster, and only on the
  receiver's lower part, in proportion to the caster's size. Before this they were "camo
  streaks". They're still flat stepped shapes (open item).
- **Wet stone at the waterline:** the wet ramp is darker with a little more chroma, and the
  stone gets one glint (1–2 px, near white) high on the lit face. At the waterline the stone
  sits on a dead-flat run with its darkest step, and there's a **continuous** 1-px lap of
  light, broken only at the sides of stones that cross the surface. (A randomly broken lap
  read as Morse code; the channel student hit the same thing.) The submerged part shows as a
  darker ghost that fades with depth. The reflection mirrors everything above the surface
  for a few rows, **in each pixel's own ramp** with the range compressed (lights drop two
  steps, darks stay), the underside row darkest, and streak rows cut through it.

---

## Stage 5: the bar surface

`bar.py`, `stage5.py`.

![bar](img/stage5_bar.png)
![head](img/stage5_bar_zoom_head.png)
![far](img/stage5_bar_zoom_far.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage5.png)

- **Size = world size × depth scale.** World size is sorted along the bar: coarse at the head
  (left) and fine at the tail (right), lognormal around the local median.
- **Three mark classes by pixel size, with a clean hand-off:**
  - STONE (≥ 5 px): the full painter.
  - TICK (2–5 px): a lozenge stamp with a light top row, a dark bottom row, 45° ends and a
    shadow-side turn.
  - GRAIN (< 2 px): a stipple of adjacent steps, a lit grain over a dark void, with contrast
    falling with distance.
- **The matrix of a clast-supported bar is dark** (voids between grains), not light sand.
  Once the matrix was light tan, the far bar read as stones sprinkled on a beach. With the
  matrix the value of the stones' voids, the stones merge into one mass with distance.
- The ground ramp has to be the *average* of the rocks (grey-green here). A warm tan made
  the far bar a different material.

The critic's words: "the first cobbles that look like the Hope and Kuban photos at a glance".
At 8×, see the open list.

---

## Stage 6: edges

`edges.py`, `stage6.py`.

![edges](img/stage6_edges.png)
![grass](img/stage6_grass_zoom.png)
![water](img/stage6_water_zoom.png)
![compare](img/compare_stage6.png)

- **Water edge.** Moving down, the bands are:
  - **damp band:** the matrix a step darker and the stones wet, 2–4 rows;
  - **waterline:** long flat runs stepping in clean runs where the bar lobes (1:1 zigzags
    looked artificial), with the continuous lap;
  - **mirror band:** a few rows reflecting the bank;
  - **open water:** 1-row dash lines.
  Shallow bed-gravel ghosting read as fish scales, so it's limited to 3 rows.
- **Grass edge.** Never a line, always a fringe:
  - the sod body ends 2 rows above the gravel;
  - tufts of 3–6 blades fan from one foot 3–7 rows *below* the edge, so the tips break up
    over the stones;
  - a few colonising tufts sit farther into the gravel;
  - the ground under the sod is shaded;
  - blades are clean runs with a dark gap on their shadow side;
  - the sod body is olive with lit blades gathered in clumps. The first version was an
    evenly spaced barcode.
- Honest: the water is far plainer than Ferrari's, which reflects the whole world in rich
  detail. Mine is a flat sky-hued field with a short bank reflection. The colonising tufts
  read as small ochre dashes. I didn't paint a scrub/willow edge (the willow element is a
  separate student's).

---

## Stage 7: the technique as code, and sheets

`cobbles_api.py`:
`paint_patch(W, H, rock, size_m, wet, density, sun, hour, px_per_stone, seed, depth_ratio)`.
`stage7.py` renders the sheets on 4 processes.

![A](img/sheet_A_hour_x_distance.png)
![B](img/sheet_B_rock_x_wetness.png)
![C](img/sheet_C_density_x_seed.png)
![D](img/sheet_D_light_direction.png)

- **A**, hour × distance (40 → 2.5 px per stone). The 20, 10 and 5 columns are the best
  cells I made. At 2.5 px it reads as gravel. At 40 px the neighbour-shadow hooks show.
- **B**, rock × wetness. Damp is chosen per stone (never a ruled boundary). Wet greywacke
  veins reach the near-white glint step, which is defensible (wet quartz) but busy.
- **C**, packing density × seed. Density 0.45 gives open-work gravel with a matrix between.
- **D**, light direction × palette.

---

## What I would teach from this

1. **Copy first, then walk to the subject.** Every rule that works in the bar came from the
   stage-0 grid reading. The rules I invented without a target (flat cast shadows, per-stone
   reflections) are the ones that still look programmatic.
2. **Fix the form, not the paint.** Eggs, moon-phase terminators and pegboard faces all went
   away when the *3D shape* became lopsided and faceted and the face mapping became banded.
3. **Darkness only where light can't reach.** Pockets under and between stones, and joins on
   the shadow side. Never an outline. This single rule separates a pile from stained glass.
4. **The palette is the light.** One index map, many hours.
5. **Mark class by pixel size.** Stone → tick → grain, with the matrix value equal to the
   voids so the hand-off doesn't pop.

## Open problems (not solved; this is why it isn't at his bar yet)

- The stage-0 crescent is concentric bands; his rim is staggered chunks with the dome's
  checker spilling in.
- Cast shadows (on neighbours and on the ground at low sun) are flat stepped shapes. They
  should wrap the receiver's form and be painted, maybe as a checker of the shadow pair like
  his 2/6 cast.
- At 40 px, a neighbour's shadow still lands as a dark hook mid-face.
- Half-buried pale stones can show as thin crescents with no body (critic, round 4).
- At golden hour the pile collapses into dark. Each stone should keep a lit sliver on its sun
  edge.
- Water: plain. It needs a richer mirror (the far bank, the sky gradient) and a sheen band.
- The 10 px stone reads as a hat.
- Speed: about 50–70 s for a 240×140 bar (1.5k stones, 3D form per stone). It's fine for
  sheets but needs caching or sprite reuse for a game.

## Tools / notes for the studio

- **numpy's threaded BLAS made small matmuls 10–20× slower here** (contention).
  `run.sh` sets `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1` etc. Worth doing studio-wide.
- `imgfetch` Wikimedia downloads hit 429s quickly, so `--src openverse` was reliable.
- Would help: a higher-resolution source of Ferrari's *wet* stones or river gravel (none of
  the Living Worlds scenes has a pebble beach seen close). V29's beach and the snow-forest
  boulders are the nearest.

## Files

- `stone.py`: the stone painter (face, crown, crescent, rim, bottom, shadow family,
  small-size lozenge), silhouettes, 3D form field, ground and cast shadow.
- `cobble.py`: the 3D lumpy faceted superellipsoid and its rasteriser.
- `materials.py`: granite, greywacke and basalt marks.
- `palette.py`: rock, gravel, water and grass ramps per hour.
- `scene.py`: multi-stone composition (occlusion, joins, cast shadows).
- `water.py`: the waterline.
- `bar.py`: the bar packer and mark classes.
- `edges.py`: the grass fringe and clean-run edges.
- `cobbles_api.py`: the one-call technique.
- `stage0_copy.py` … `stage7.py`, `compare_sheets.py`: stage images and sheets.
- `refs/`: fetched photos (with `sources.tsv`).
- `scratch/`: analysis grids and throwaway tests.
